The England captain writhed in pain and, although he gingerly got back to his feet and initially soldiered on, it was clear he could not continue and he hobbled off the field alongside two physios after treatment.

Kane had a quick exchange with Vincent Kompany, who was back in the Bayern hot-seat for the first time in six matches after being diagnosed with meningeal irritation last month, before heading down the tunnel.

After the match Bayern confirmed Kane had torn the syndesmotic ligament and "will therefore be sidelined for the next few months".

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Kane earlier this month signed a new deal with the Bundesliga giants until June 2029 - reportedly worth in excess of £400,000 a week, making him the highest-paid player in Europe before bonuses.

Since arriving from Tottenham three years ago, Kane has bagged a scarcely believable 156 goals in 163 appearances in all competitions, helping Bayern win a league and cup double last season.

He went into the international break having a drawn a blank in his four previous matches for Bayern but he scored twice for England, including from the spot in their 3-2 Nations League win over Spain.

But after being forced off after 20 minutes at the Allianz Arena, replaced by Tom Bischof, Kane could now be facing a battle to be fully fit for England's two-legged quarter-final against Norway in March.

A three-month period on the sidelines would rule Kane out for 11 Bundesliga games, the DFB-Pokal round of 16 tie against Hertha Berlin, as well as all four of Bayern's remaining Champions League league phase matches.

Providing his recovery goes to plan, that would give the striker just over a month to prepare for England's Nations League quarter-final.

The Three Lions face Norway in Oslo in the first leg on March 27, before the return leg at Wembley on March 30.

Henry Winter speaking on Back Pages Tonight:

"I've covered Harry Kane's career for so many years and there was a period where he had almost a chronic problem with his ankles.

"It's three months and, most immediately, it's Bayern Munich's concern. But England have the home and away Nations League quarter-finals against Norway in late March.

"The timeline would suggest he'll be back in that time, but thoughts with Thomas Tuchel tonight because he'll be absolutely concerned about this.

"He's such an important player. England's No 9. There's a bit of a gap behind him if you're looking for No 9s. I know Jude Bellingham can play there and that's probably the long-term plan. But this is hugely alarming."

If AI Chips Become Commoditized, Nvidia Has the Most to Lose, While These 3 Stocks Are Set to Gain

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia has a lot to lose as AI chips become fungible, while these stocks could gain.

By Geoffrey Seiler – Nov 21, 2026 at 6:15AM EST

The AI chip market is starting to shift, and with it, Nvidia's (NVDA -14.01%) grip. Anthropic recently revealed that its Claude Mythos model could start running part of its AI models on alternative AI chips to Nvidia's graphics processing units (GPUs) in just a few days with basically no human intervention, a process that used to take six to 12 months and dozens of engineers. This greatly reduces Nvidia's CUDA moat at a time when competitors are starting to catch up.

Alphabet's (GOOGL -2.70%) (GOOG -2.59%) Tensor Processing Units (TPUs) have started to outperform Nvidia's GPUs on a performance-per-dollar basis, according to Semianalysis (though some dispute the results). Meanwhile, Advanced Micro Devices' (AMD -13.25%) GPUs continue to gain traction. The company has improved its software stack, and its chips' larger memory capacity makes them ideal for inference.

If the gap between AI chips continues to shrink and AI models can be quickly shifted to run on any AI accelerator, this is very bad news for Nvidia. Not only will the company continue to lose market share, but, more importantly, its pricing power will erode, and its margins will shrink. The Nvidia premium can be seen in its gross margins, which are in the mid-70% range, versus AMD’s in the mid-50% range. Meanwhile, all of Nvidia's largest customers, outside of SpaceX (SPCX -7.69%), have been developing their own custom AI chips to try to break free of Nvidia's high prices.

All is not lost for Nvidia, as the company still holds some advantages. It has created a cohesive hardware, software, and networking ecosystem that should still be able to command a premium. It also has major current supply chain advantages, having locked up more access to constrained high bandwidth memory (HBM) supply and having more manufacturing and packaging capacity with Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (TSM -9.48%). That will help it maintain its leadership position, even as it will likely face continued market-share and pricing pressure.

While Nvidia may struggle, these three AI stocks look set to benefit.

Lower AI accelerator prices and more Nvidia GPU alternatives could boost overall AI chip demand, which would benefit Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing. It would also lower TSMC's customer concentration and could actually help with pricing power and gross margins.

Whether a hyperscaler, neocloud, enterprise, or government buys Nvidia GPUs, AMD accelerators, or ASICs through a company like Broadcom (AVGO -12.19%) or Marvell Technology (MRVL -11.69%), TSMC still wins at the end of the day. That makes the stock a buy.

As both a cloud computing giant and a developer of TPUs, Alphabet is one of the best-positioned companies to benefit from the commoditization of AI chips. Alphabet has more than a decade's head start with custom chips and is creating an ecosystem to rival chipmakers like Nvidia and AMD. This lets it get better utilization out of its data centers and gives it an advantage over other companies using their own custom AI chips.

Meanwhile, because its offerings cost much less than Nvidia's, it should be able to gain share with external customers without sacrificing margins. This ultimately could make Alphabet one of the biggest winners in the AI space during this transition.

Starting from a much smaller market-share base with an operating model already built around slimmer gross margins, AMD should benefit as AI chips become more commoditized. Between its lower prices, improving cost performance, and the recent introduction of a complete AI rack system (Helios), AI chip pricing normalization and chip fungibility actually become a tailwind rather than a headwind for a smaller Nvidia competitor like AMD.

Meanwhile, the market is heading in AMD's direction with inference and agentic AI, with customers caring far more about memory capacity, central processing unit (CPU) integrations, and cost-per-action, which is where its chips shine. With its new Helios system, which combines its GPUs and CPUs, AMD has a big opportunity to establish itself as a meaningful alternative to Nvidia in the coming years. This makes the stock a buy.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 768%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 199% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

L'ex Jungdal: “Allenarsi con Maignan e Donnarumma è stata la miglior scuola possibile. Tresoldi? Un grande. Prendetelo!”

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Quanti possono dire di essersi allenati al fianco di Gianluigi Donnarumma e Mike Maignan quando erano ancora giovanissimi? Andreas Jungdal è uno di questi. Arrivato al Milan nel 2019 quando aveva appena 17 anni, il portiere danese ha avuto il privilegio di condividere il campo con due dei migliori estremi difensori della sua generazione, imparando anche da una leggenda rossonera come Nelson Dida. Oggi, dopo aver intrapreso il proprio percorso lontano da Milano, continua a seguire con affetto il club che tifava da bambino. In esclusiva ai microfoni di MilanNews, nell’intervista realizzata dal giornalista Alessandro Schiavone, l'attuale portiere titolare del K.V.C Westerlo in Belgio e riserva della nazionale danese ripercorre gli anni in rossonero, mette a confronto Donnarumma e Maignan, racconta il rapporto con Dida e si sofferma anche su Nicolò Tresoldi, l’attaccante italo-tedesco che sta brillando in Belgio e che consiglierebbe senza esitazioni al Milan. Protagonista di una grande serata, con un rigore parato. Hai imparato da Gigio come si parano i rigori? “Sì, si può dire così (ride, ndr). Vabbè, ho imparato tanto da Gigio e pure da Mike. Insomma, da tutti i portieri del Milan! Allenarmi con loro due è stata una vera scuola per portieri.”   Hai imparato di più da Maignan o da Donnarumma?  “Vabbè, sono due portieri molto diversi. Secondo me Gigio sulla linea è un portiere incredibile, mentre Mike magari con la palla è più forte. Hanno qualità diverse e sono stato fortunato a imparare da tutti e due.”   Ti sei allenato due anni con Donnarumma, dal 2019 al 2021, e 18 mesi con Mike Maignan, dall'estate 2021 al gennaio 2023. Puoi dirci chi è più forte?  “Mah, io non posso dire chi è più forte (ride, ndr).  Ma secondo me sulla linea Gigio è il numero uno al mondo! Ha un fisico enorme e un’esplosività impressionante. Però Mike è al Milan da oltre cinque anni ormai e ha vinto pure il campionato! Anche qui parliamo di un campione e lo ha dimostrato anche con la nazionale francese, facendo molto, molto bene…”   In cuor tuo, quando Donnarumma decise di non rinnovare il contratto e di andare al PSG a parametro zero, non speravi di diventare il portiere titolare del Milan?  “Sì, certo che ci speravo, ma ero molto, molto giovane. Poi ero il terzo o quarto portiere. Se vuoi diventare il titolare del Milan devi essere un campione! Hanno preso Mike, ma io sono felice dei miei anni in rossonero. Va bene così.”   La scuola italiana dei portieri è così forte come si dice?  “L’Italia ha fatto la storia sfornando portieri di grande livello, soprattutto tecnicamente. Lì sono fissati con la tecnica… come pari la palla e come muovi i piedi. E tutte quelle cose lì… Credo che per un portiere giovane quella italiana sia una scuola fantastica."   Ma come ti ha scoperto il Milan?  "Penso tramite lo scouting… Io avevo mosso i primi passi al Vejle. Ho fatto un anno in nazionale nell’Under 17 e penso che mi abbiano scoperto lì. Ma sinceramente non sono sicurissimo. Sono stato fortunato ad andare lì e seguo tuttora il Milan. È il club per il quale tifavo da piccolo. È un grande club a livello mondiale che tifo ancora. Auguro tutto il meglio al Milan.”   Il tuo preparatore dei portieri è stato niente meno che mister Nelson Dida, vincitore di due Champions League e portiere del Grande Milan.  “Innanzitutto è una persona straordinaria! Se non sbaglio è passato dalla Primavera alla prima squadra. Anche da lui ho imparato tantissimo, così come da Gigi Ragno ed Emiliano Betti, che erano anche loro preparatori dei portieri. Però che feeling con Dida, che era una vera e propria leggenda. Sono stati bei tempi…”   Tresoldi, tra andata e ritorno fra Bruges e Westerlo nella passata stagione, ti ha segnato tre gol. Il Milan starebbe pensando di prenderlo a gennaio: farebbe bene a puntare sull’italo-tedesco? “Assolutamente sì! Lui qui in Belgio sta facendo davvero bene, è sempre tra i capocannonieri del campionato. È ancora molto giovane, ma sta facendo dei passi impressionanti. Il Milan farà bene a prenderlo.”   È quel tipo di attaccante che magari non vedi per 89 minuti e partecipa poco al gioco... e poi al 90’ la butta dentro. Sei d’accordo?  “Esatto… quando deve essere lì, è là… davanti alla porta. In fase di realizzazione è davvero forte! Il Bruges ha una squadra forte che lo manda sempre in porta! Nicolò è davvero forte!”   Quando vedi Torriani giocare in prima squadra di tanto in tanto, non provi un po’ d’invidia? Magari con un pizzico di fortuna in più avresti potuto esserci tu al suo posto…  “Sì, ma nel calcio, così come nella vita, le cose vanno come devono andare. Lui ha avuto anche adesso delle occasioni. Sono felice per lui, anche perché abbiamo fatto un po’ lo stesso percorso nel Milan. Quando lo vedo giocare sono felice per lui.”   Sogni di tornare al Milan da protagonista un giorno? Magari come un certo Dida che dopo la papera di Leeds lasciò in prestito prima di tornare e diventare il miglior portiere al mondo dal 2002 al 2005...   “Mah, certo! È un sogno e ogni giocatore sogna di giocare per una grande squadra come il Milan. È un sogno che ho da bambino… Forza Milan!”

European-Japanese BepiColombo probe arrives in orbit around Mercury today

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Stargazing tips, cosmic events and expert insights on the universe

Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

It's a moment more than eight years in the making.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Mercury is about to receive a robotic visitor from Earth.

The BepiColombo mission — a joint effort of the European Space Agency (ESA) and the Japanese Aerospace Exploration Agency (JAXA) — will arrive in orbit around Mercury today (Nov. 21), more than eight years after leaving its home planet.

The big moment will come after an orbit-insertion burn, which is scheduled to begin today at 1:24 p.m. EST (1824 GMT) and last for 37 minutes.

BepiColombo consists of two main spacecraft: ESA's Mercury Planetary Orbiter (MPO) and JAXA's Mercury Magnetospheric Orbiter (MMO), also known as Mio.

The duo launched — along with a power and propulsion element called the Mercury Transfer Module (MTM) — aboard an Ariane 5 rocket from French Guiana in October 2018, beginning a long and looping trip through the inner solar system.

Mercury is a difficult planet to reach because it's so close to the sun. Spacecraft approaching the solar system's innermost planet are accelerated to high velocities by the immense gravitational pull of our star and must shed a great deal of kinetic energy to be captured by Mercury's much weaker tug.

So the BepiColombo team designed a trajectory that featured one flyby of Earth, two of Venus and six of Mercury over the course of about seven years. That flight time was increased by a year, however, after the mission suffered an issue with its solar panels, which reduced the power available for the MTM's electric thrusters.

Today's orbital insertion is the latest milestone in BepiColombo's Mercury arrival phase, which is expected to take about six months in total.

Arrival began on Sept. 3, when the MTM released MPO and Mio. The two orbiters remain connected today; they won't separate until Dec. 9 or thereabouts.

Shortly thereafter, Mio will settle into its final orbit, a highly elliptical path that will take it over Mercury's poles. MPO is also destined for a polar orbit, though a much less elliptical one than Mio's; the ESA probe will conduct a series of engine burns before finally getting there on March 10, 2027.

BepiColombo won't start doing science work until April 6, 2027. The two probes will gather complementary data from their two different vantage points, shedding a great deal of light on a mysterious world.

"The mission will study all aspects of Mercury, from the structure and dynamics of its magnetosphere and how it interacts with the solar wind, to its internal structure with its large iron core, and the origin of the planet’s magnetic field," ESA officials wrote in a BepiColombo mission description.

"The data will enable scientists to understand more about the origin and evolution of a planet located close to its parent star, and a better understanding of the overall evolution of our solar system," they added.

Michael Wall is the Spaceflight and Tech Editor for Space.com and joined the team in 2010. He primarily covers human and robotic spaceflight, military space, and exoplanets, but has been known to dabble in the space art beat. His book about the search for alien life, "Out There," was published on Nov. 13, 2018. Before becoming a science writer, Michael worked as a herpetologist and wildlife biologist. He has a Ph.D. in evolutionary biology from the University of Sydney, Australia, a bachelor's degree from the University of Arizona, and a graduate certificate in science writing from the University of California, Santa Cruz. To find out what his latest project is, you can follow Michael on Twitter.

Kieran Gibbs recalls his infamous Chelsea red card

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A case of mistaken identity sparked one of the most bizarre Premier League incidents ever

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Controversial refereeing decisions have been a part of football for a long as the game has been around, but one of the most bizarre incidents in recent memory occurred during Arsenal’s infamous 6-0 defeat to Chelsea in March 2014.

After spotting an Arsenal handball on the goal line, referee Andrer Marriner reached for his pocket and flashed a red card to Kieran Gibbs.

However, the issue here was that it was actually Alex Oxlade-Chamberlain who had handled the ball, and in this pre-VAR era, Gibbs was forced into an early bath, despite the protests of players from both sides.

As Gibbs looks back on the incident more than a decade on, he remembers the confusion, the frustration and the surreal nature of the incident which wouldn’t be allowed to happen these days.

“It feels like a blur now, and it did at the time too,” he reflects to FourFourTwo. “It’s heated in the moment and you’re trying to focus on the game.”

The incident came during a calamitous first half for the Gunners, who found themselves 2-0 down after just seven minutes in what was Arsene Wenger’s 1,000th game in charge of the club.

The handball incident came in the 17th minute, when Oxlade-Chamberlain threw his left hand at an Eden Hazard shot, tipping it around the post. Marriner pointed to the spot and the referee was not for turning, despite widespread protests from both sets of players.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

The Belgian would slot home the penalty to effectively end the game as a contest, with Gibbs still finding it remarkable that such an incident would occur, given the technology now used in the game.

“It feels crazy that there was no VAR, because you don’t have those kinds of issues anymore,” he continues.

“It was a surreal and very frustrating moment that was unique in the Premier League, so it brought a lot of attention.

“Both captains and the referee were telling me I was definitely going off.

“After a few minutes of protesting, I needed to listen to them.

“It was a big error from the referee, who apologised later and the red card was rescinded.”

For more than a decade, Joe Mewis has worked in football journalism as a reporter and editor. Mewis has had stints at Mirror Football and LeedsLive among others and worked at FourFourTwo throughout Euro 2024, reporting on the tournament. In addition to his journalist work, Mewis is also the author of four football history books that include times on Leeds United and the England national team. Now working as a digital marketing coordinator at Harrogate Town, too, Mewis counts some of his best career moments as being in the iconic Spygate press conference under Marcelo Bielsa and seeing his beloved Leeds lift the Championship trophy during lockdown.

The best Kindles of 2026: We tested Amazon’s new e-readers to find the winners

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In October, Amazon launched a brand-new round of Kindle e-readers, making it even harder to pick one. It doesn't help that the new 2026 Kindles are more expensive, too. I've spent years testing Kindles for Mashable, and I can tell you that the latest batch is impressive, and it shows exactly why Kindles are still the most popular e-readers.

After testing the newest Kindles, we have a new favorite. The Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition has edged out the standard Paperwhite as the best Kindle to buy in 2026. However, that doesn't mean it's the best Kindle for everyone, and so we've tested the whole Kindle lineup to make expert recommendations for every type of reader.

These are the tech, tools, and products — from laptops to e-readers, from earbuds to robovacs, and more — that Mashable ranks best in class.

Once you have a Kindle, there's still so much you can do to make the most of it. For avid readers, Stuff Your Kindle Day and Libby let you cross every last book off your annual reading list. You can even buy Kindle accessories like remote page-turners and gooseneck stands so you don't have to hold your Kindle while reading. (By the way, we love Amazon's new Kindle Click and Page Turn Cover, and the latter even reminds us of the cult-favorite Kindle Oasis, RIP.)

So, after extensive testing, reading so many books, checking in with BookTok authors, and attending BookCon, I created this guide to the best Kindles of 2026. Plus, with Black Friday coming up, I've found the best deals on Kindles.

Yes, Kindles are on sale for the Amazon Black Friday sale, so if you're buying an e-reader as a Christmas gift, this is your chance to save. Amazon kicked off its Black Friday sale on Nov. 19, and with it, all of the 2024 Kindle models dropped in price. The best deal is on the 2024 Kindle Paperwhite, one of our favorites. The 2024 e-reader saw a $50 price increase recently, but now it's down to $129.99, which is even cheaper than before. But there are plenty of other Kindle models on sale, including the 2024 Kindle Colorsoft (now $120 off) and the Kindle Scribe (now $150 off). However, it's only 2024 models on sale so far, as Amazon hasn't marked down its new 2026 models during Black Friday.

Fortunately, we still love the 2024 Kindles, and they even work with the new Kindle Click.

On Oct. 1, Amazon announced a redesign of the entire Kindle collection. The new Kindles have vivid new colors like ube, and for the first time, you can buy these e-readers with aluminum casings. The new Kindles are also thinner and lighter than before. They also have a removable back panel so you can eventually replace the battery, though we have had some problems with this feature.

Most readers: Amazon's latest release, the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition, is the best Kindle. While we previously saw it as an upgrade pick, the new aluminum casing makes it essential with a sleeker, sturdier case. Inside, it's still the same speedy Paperwhite we love. It turns pages without stuttering, never slowing down when you’re in the thick of the action.

People who read on the go: The 7-inch screen has adjustable brightness and warmth but it also auto-adjusts. That means if you're commuting or the sun changes overhead, the Paperwhite Signature Edition will adjust the screen based on your environment. The 2026 model is lighter and thinner than ever and fits comfortably in small crossbody bags. Plus, it’s waterproof, keeping it safe from poolside vacations and spilled water bottles in bags.

When picking the best Kindle, it always comes down to this question — Paperwhite or Paperwhite Signature Edition? Historically, I've preferred the Paperwhite, finding that the Signature Edition doesn't have enough features to justify its price. The 2026 Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition finally fixes that. With a new aluminum case, the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition feels like a high-end piece of tech. With a thinner body and square edges, this new Kindle feels more like a smartphone or tablet than it does an e-reader.

But inside the more advanced chassis is still the same e-reader you love. It's fast with a high contrast 300ppi resolution. The matte display is glare-free and easy on the eyes. With adjustable warmth and brightness you can read with it in bright summer sun at the beach and then turn it down and warm it up when you're in a dim bedroom. And speaking of the beach, its IPX8 rating makes it safe to use in the bath or at the pool.

It is a more expensive e-reader at $249.99, especially compared to the Paperwhite's $199.99, but here's what you get for an extra $50. The upgraded aluminum case makes that $50 nearly worth it compared to the plastic exterior of the Paperwhite, but the Signature Edition also has an ad-free lockscreen, 32GB of storage compared to the Paperwhite's 16GB, wireless charging, auto-adjusting brightness, and best of all, compatibility with the new Kindle Page Turn Cover.

Plus, the Kindle Paperwhite Signature comes in more exciting color options. While the Paperwhite just comes in graphite, the Signature Edition also comes in a stunning seaglass green.

Budget shoppers: Especially in light of the Kindle price increases, if you want to read books on an e-reader but need a budget-friendly model, the regular Kindle is the best option. It recently got an update, too, and the 2026 Kindle is better than ever. It’s the smallest Kindle, with a 6-inch screen that makes it pocket-sized (if you have big enough pockets). But with a budget device, keep in mind you have to make some sacrifices. The basic Kindle isn't waterproof nor does it have adjustable warm light.

Amazon made some updates to the 2026 Kindle that make it worthy of its higher price tag. It has a new, flush front design for a sleeker look. Plus, the design team made a change we've been dying for — they moved the power button to the side of the device. Plenty of specs have stayed the same between generations. It still has a 6-inch screen, 300 ppi resolution, and six weeks of battery life.

What truly makes it stand out is just how lightweight it is. The 2026 Kindle weighs 18 grams (.66 ounces) less than the previous generation and it's a noticeable difference. It feels like you're barely holding anything; plus, it's even better for travel as it takes up very little space and weighs just half a pound.

It's a speedy little e-reader, too. Its screen is responsive and loads books within seconds. The only thing that could make the e-reader better is if Amazon added an adjustable warm light and made it waterproof. However, according to Amazon, the lack of those features keeps it affordable.

The device is now available in its basic format in either ube or graphite. However, it's also available with an aluminum backing, making it more like a tablet. The aluminum Kindle is more expensive at $189.99, but it does come with 32GB of storage.

Read our full review of the Kindle Paperwhite (2024).

If you don't like the new prices for Amazon's new 2026 Kindles, then you can still buy the 2024 Kindle Paperwhite. Up until October, this was our pick as the best Kindle, and it's just as good now. Best of all, during big sales like Black Friday, you can pick up this popular e-reader for $129.99. The new model costs $199.99, which is a big ask for most readers.

The screen's front light is vibrant and has adjustable brightness, meaning it can adjust when transitioning from reading in the sun or shade. It also comes with adjustable screen warmth, perfect for evening readers who want to give their eyes a chance to relax. It’s lightweight and fits comfortably in small crossbody bags. Plus, it’s waterproof, keeping it safe against poolside vacations and spilled water bottles in bags.

I used this Kindle for two years, and it's as good as the day I took it out of the box. Plus, it doesn't suffer from the back cover design flaw readers have said they've encountered with the 2026 Kindle e-readers.

There's a recurring question we see about Kindles: What’s the difference between the Kindle Paperwhite and Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition? The Signature Edition has twice the storage of the Paperwhite (32GB), auto-adjusting screen brightness, an ad-free lockscreen, and wireless charging, and it costs $40 more. Sure, those things are helpful, but I don’t think those extra features are necessary.

Not a ton changed between the 2022 and 2024 models of the Kindle Paperwhite, but we can be certain that the 2024 Kindle Paperwhite is the sleeker, faster e-reader. The 2024 Paperwhite got a slight upgrade in screen quality compared to the 2022 Paperwhite. While the resolution is still 300 ppi, it appears to have higher contrast, straying away from the like-paper look and edging closer to the look of a tablet. Don’t worry, though — unlike tablets, the Kindle Paperwhite still has a gloriously glare-free screen. It still has an IPX68 waterproof rating, so you can get some leisurely reading in the bath.

Note: On Oct. 1, 2026, Amazon released a new version of the Kindle Paperwhite Kids. We are currently in the process of testing the new e-reader.

Young readers: If you're raising a voracious reader, the Kindle Paperwhite Kids is the best Kindle for kids. It's waterproof, which means it stands up to juice spills and dips in the pool. Plus, it comes with a Kindle case included. It's designed to be kid-proof, not just physically, but also with built-in parental controls. If you're concerned about monitoring your kid's bookshelf, this Kindle allows you to approve downloads by kids and displays age-friendly titles that kids can easily access.

More than anything else, a Kindle for kids has to be waterproof, which is why the Kindle Paperwhite Kids is the best choice. It comes with everything we like about the standard Paperwhite: a beautiful, high-resolution screen that works faster than any previous generation.

But what makes it great for kids is that it's a distraction-free device. It doesn't come with ads, notifications, or the temptation of games or video. I also like that it's a bit heavier than the standard Paperwhite, giving it a sturdier design. Plus, the built-in case adds an extra level of protection.

The parental controls are easy to use and set up, and the device also comes with a six-month free subscription to Amazon Kids+, which provides unlimited access to thousands of age-appropriate books. The library of books is easy for kids to navigate and offers classics like Lois Lowry's The Giver and modern classics like Diary of a Wimpy Kid.

What I don't love is the price, especially now that it just rose by $50. At $229.99, it's more expensive than the standard Paperwhite. If you want to go for a budget kids' pick, I'd go for a standard Kindle, which you can set up as a kids' device during setup to ensure parental controls and add an Amazon Kids+ subscription for $5.99 per month. Plus, as your kid ages, you won't have to upgrade to a more teen-friendly e-reader.

For color enthusiasts: The Kindle Colorsoft would probably be a better option for kids than for adults, serving as a bridge between colorful storybooks and more challenging chapter books. But it works for adults too, especially those who want color or to use the Page Turn Cover.

The Kindle Colorsoft makes some modest improvements over its predecessor, introducing the same design changes as the rest of Amazon's 2026 Kindle lineup. That means the new Kindle Colorsoft has a slimmer build, magnetic Pogo pins, and edge-to-edge color, so the bezels now match the device's color. Overall, it's an improvement, but it doesn't solve the problems of the original device, nor fully justify the $289.99 price (or $319.99 for the Signature Edition).

Unfortunately, E Ink technology has extremely dim and muted colors. Even if you crank the vibrancy settings all the way to the max, the colors are disappointing. When you compare the Kindle Colorsoft side-by-side with graphic novels or books with illustrations, the differences are extremely obvious. If you want to read comics or similar color artwork digitally, you're better off with a tablet.

With those major caveats, if you want a Kindle with color, this is the best option. On the plus side, we love the fig colorway (it matches the burgundy iPhone), and the color lockscreen artwork does look great.

We have yet to thoroughly vet the new Kindle Paperwhite, but before the device's release, we got a sneak peek of the new models. We were impressed by the new aluminum models of the Kindle Paperwhite. Plus, I'm pleased to see that the power button has been moved to the side of the device. Based on how much we've loved previous generations of the Kindle Paperwhite, we feel comfortable saying the new gen will be a good purchase.

Read our full review of the Kindle Scribe (3rd Generation).

Note takers and annotators: If, looking through your bookshelf, your books have dog-eared pages, highlighted lines, and scribbles in the margins, your instinct might be that an e-reader isn't for you, but I'd like to introduce you to the Kindle Scribe. It's the only Kindle with writing capabilities and comes with an included stylus pen that magnetically attaches to the side of the e-reader; it's ready for all your handwritten thoughts.

The pen moves nicely across the screen, mimicking the texture of writing on paper. Its larger screen also makes it better for writing and reading PDFs.

In addition to a library of books, the Kindle Scribe also comes with a journal section where you can take notes. The notes feature is especially great for students who want to handwrite notes, convert their handwriting into text, and manage documents through Google Drive.

The 2025 Kindle Scribe got a much-needed upgrade. All the good stuff has held over with the addition of some major improvements.

It's still big, with an 11-inch display, which does make it a little more awkward to hold while reading, but for writing or annotating, it's optimal. Unlike other tablets (I'm looking at you, iPads) that require you to buy a pen separately, the Kindle Scribe comes with a pen that conveniently attaches to the side so it doesn't get lost. However, it's not the most secure magnetic hold, so be careful not to bump the pen off accidentally.

Writing on the Kindle Scribe is a joy. Before testing this e-reader, I used my iPad and Apple Pencil to annotate readings, but I never loved it because the screen felt too slick for the Apple Pencil. The Kindle Scribe does not suffer this problem, as the matte screen lends some friction to the pen, making it feel like you're writing on paper. I enjoyed writing with the pen, and I swear my handwriting looked better on the Kindle Scribe than on my iPad.

It finally got a processing update that makes it as speedy as every other Kindle. Gone is the lag when switching between journals and books. Plus, the displays have greatly improved, too, as the 2025 Kindle Scribe has virtually no ghosting. Like the Kindle Paperwhite, the Scribe's battery life lasts 12 weeks. It also has multiple storage options, as you can choose between 32GB or 64GB.

It's not your only option if you want a Kindle that can write. The Scribe Colorsoft is also available, and while we appreciate that model, it's quite a bit more expensive. The 2025 Scribe is a better value, it just doesn't have the wow factor of color.

For years, Kindle prices have stayed stable, but in August 2026, Amazon finally increased prices. Every model except the Kindle Scribe received a price hike. We can thank RAMageddon, as an Amazon spokesperson told Mashable that the increases were necessary due to rising memory and component costs.

Our advice? If you're on a budget, the 2024 Kindles are still very good e-readers.

If you're concerned about buying the newest tech, that's not an issue since, as of October 2026, there are nine new models to shop.

Before you choose a Kindle, you need to ask one question first: Do you want it to be waterproof? If you prefer reading near a body of water — whether in the bath, by the pool, or at the beach — that immediately narrows down your options. Since the Kindle Oasis is no longer available, your waterproof options are the Kindle Paperwhite, Paperwhite Signature Edition, Colorsoft Signature Edition, and Paperwhite Kids.

Beyond water resistance, here are a few other features to consider:

Display size: The basic Kindle has a 6-inch display, while the largest has a 10.2-inch display.

Storage capacity: Kindles range from 16GB to 32GB of storage capacity.

Battery life: The new Kindles generally have outstanding batteries. On the low end, they last up to six weeks per charge, while others last up to 12 weeks.

Writing capability: The Kindle Scribe is the only model that allows you to write with the included stylus.

The latest Kindles all boast a maximum brightness of 94 nits and a 300 ppi, high-resolution display with zero glare, so you can enjoy sharp text and images at any viewing angle. Kindles are also equipped with WiFi connectivity, allowing you to download all the reading material you could ever want. Plus, you can listen to audiobooks on Audible via the built-in Bluetooth connection.

And if you were a die-hard Kindle Oasis fan and are attached to page-turning buttons, your best option is to get the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition with the Page Turn Cover.

Out of all the e-readers I've tested (and I've tested a lot), Kindles have the best compatibility with Libby. I love the digital platform because I can read for free and support my local library. Some other e-readers require you to download your library e-book to a computer and then upload it to an e-reader, but with Kindles, your e-books are sent straight to your device with a click.

Libby allows you to read free e-books on a Kindle. Through Libby, you can borrow e-books through libraries, and all you need is a library card. Inside the Libby app, select the "Read with Kindle" option when you borrow a book, which allows you to send these e-books to your Kindle library.

Another option for finding free e-books is through Stuff Your Kindle events. These are author-driven events that offer limited-time deals on e-books, often making them heavily discounted or totally free.

The Mashable team is split on this question. Our resident Kindle expert Samantha Mangino (yours truly) recommends most people opt for the ad-supported version. However, Mashable Tech Editor Timothy Beck Werth recommends paying a little more to get a Kindle without lockscreen ads. Though this increases the price by $20 to $25, you won't be subjected to annoying ads for books every time you lock your e-reader. Instead, you'll see black-and-white photographs of writing implements and other book motifs.

When you're buying a Kindle, you'll notice that Amazon selects the "Lockscreen Ad Supported" version by default. Shoppers must manually select "Without Lockscreen Ads" if they want to remove advertisements from the lock screen. This increases the cost of the base Kindle from $149.99 to $169.99; the Kindle Paperwhite increases from $199.99 to $219.99. For serious readers who hate ads, we think it's worth the added cost.

In the past, the Paperwhite has been Amazon's flagship e-reader, with the Paperwhite Signature Edition being the more luxe option. However, with the 2026 redesign, it seems like the script has been flipped, with Amazon pushing the Signature Edition while the standard Paperwhite takes a back seat.

Plenty remains the same between the devices. They have the same 7-inch screen with 300 ppi resolution and adjustable warmth and brightness. Both are waterproof with IPX8 ratings and 12-week battery life. The new devices also have easy-to-open back panels to make replacing the battery that much easier.

The Paperwhite is the cheaper option at $199.99, but it comes with only 16GB of storage (which, to be fair, is still plenty) and is available in graphite with a plastic casing. The Signature Edition is more expensive at $249.99, but comes with 32GB of storage and is available in the aluminum finish in seaglass green and graphite. Beyond its nicer finish, it also has an ad-free lockscreen, automatic front-light adjustment, a rotating screen, and wireless charging. The real perk of the Paperwhite Signature Edition is its compatibility with the Page Turn Cover, which adds page-turning buttons to your Kindle.

As of May 20, 2026, Amazon no longer provides software updates or web services to devices released in 2012 or earlier, essentially bricking them. Users will still be able to read currently downloaded books on the device, but to add new books, they will need to connect the device to a computer. This is a huge hit, especially to Libby users who need web service access to read borrowed books.

Owners of these Kindle models are obviously upset, as it means they'll need to purchase a new device to maintain access to all features. While some are considering switching to a different e-reader, like a Kobo, Amazon still makes great e-readers, and it's a testament to the brand that devices that are at least 14 years old are still going strong.

Unfortunately, while a lot of Amazon devices get regular discounts throughout the year, we only see Kindle discounts during big shopping events like Prime Day and Black Friday. However, even at full price, these devices earn a strong recommendation from Mashable.

Kindle Kids editions come with built-in parental controls. It displays a library geared toward kid-friendly and age-appropriate content. Plus, it allows your kid to request books to read that you have to approve in the Kindle app — that way, you know exactly what they're reading.

Unlike other Kindle models, the Kindle Kids includes a built-in cover and a free one-year Amazon Kids+ subscription that provides access to thousands of books for kids ages 6 to 12. Plus, it comes with a worry-free guarantee: If the Kindle Kids gets damaged within two years of purchase, they'll replace it totally free.

However, any Kindle can technically be a suitable device for kids. When setting up a Kindle, you'll receive a prompt asking if the device is for children, which enables parental controls on it.

Yes, you can buy new Kindles at Best Buy. However, if you're searching for the best price and a convenient shopping experience, we recommend sticking with Amazon. Since the Kindle store is also hosted at Amazon, you'll need an account there anyway, and it's hard to beat Amazon's prices on its own products.

Apple iPads are better than ever. We know because we've tested them all, from the older base model iPad to the brand-new iPad Mini. For shoppers suffering from decision fatigue, we can help you choose the right tablet for your budget and use case, whether that's drawing, playing games on the couch, getting some work done, or all of the above. So, based on extensive testing, we chose the best iPads of 2026.

These are the tech, tools, and products — from laptops to e-readers, from earbuds to robovacs, and more — that Mashable ranks best in class.

At this point, iPads can do almost anything your laptop can do. The new windowing and multitasking features introduced with Liquid Glass and iPadOS 26 (and recently updated in iPadOS 27) support a wide range of use cases. Not to mention, the iPad Air with M4 just got a memory boost up to 12GB, putting it a tier above many laptops. Whether you want to stream, play The Sims, browse the internet, illustrate, or edit videos, you can do it on the iPad.

But which iPad should you choose? Apple's tablet lineup can be a bit confusing, especially with the recent release of the new iPad Mini, which saw a price increase to $699, almost as much as the iPad Air. Pretty much everyone: The iPad Air is the best iPad for most people. It will be your new go-to device for streaming, reading, or creative projects. And if you plan on using your tablet as a substitute for a laptop, it'll work for that, too, especially when paired with the new Magic Keyboard for iPad Air or Apple Pencil. It's fast, moves smoothly between apps, and has a stunning high-definition display.

Creatives on a budget: While we prefer the iPad Pro for creatives, the iPad Air is a more budget-friendly option that will still handle tough design jobs, and it's compatible with the Apple Pencil Pro.

To kick off 2026, Apple upgraded the iPad Air to include the M4 chip. While the update didn't feel necessary in light of the already powerful iPad with M3 chip, we'll never say no to more computing power, especially with no price increase.

The iPad Air bridges the gap between tablet and laptop, yet it still remains affordable and user-friendly. With the M4 chip upgrade, the iPad Air got a boost, now scoring 13,176 on the Geekbench 6 test, up from the previous model's score of 11,464.

While the iPad Pro is technically slightly thinner and lighter, the iPad Air still lives up to its name. It's a great tablet to bring on the go due to its light weight, which helps avoid wrist strain when holding the device.

It has crisp displays for brilliant graphics, and one of its most surprising upgrades in the 2026 model is more memory. Now with 12GB of RAM, it's better equipped as a gaming device, too. For creatives too, the extra memory makes it even more capable of creative tasks, whether that be in Final Cut Pro or while doing design work with the Apple Pencil.

Essentially, it gives you everything you'd want in a starter MacBook, but it costs you just $749 and has a touchscreen.

Budget shoppers: If you prefer saving money over splurging on the latest tech, the Apple iPad (11th generation) gets the job done. It's the base model, meaning it won't have the fastest processor like the iPad Air's M4 chip or the latest features like Apple Intelligence. However, it is the most affordable Apple tablet. With the M4 iPad Air and M5 iPad Pro models, this iPad's price dropped to $349 — a real steal for Apple.

Even though its tech is basic compared to the advanced iPad Air and Pro, it's great for someone who wants a portable screen for streaming, playing games, and reading. It's also a nice choice if you're interested in using your tablet as a laptop, but don't want to spend a ton of money. It's compatible with the Magic Keyboard Folio and together costs $598 — the same price as the iPad Air.

Kids: It's especially great for kids if you want to get them a tablet but don't want to spend a fortune on it.

The iPad (11th generation) doesn't possess the flair of the Pro or Air, but it is a testament that Apple can still produce good entry-level tech. It's currently the lowest-priced iPad, even defeating the mini.

The 2025 iPad model got a slight chip upgrade, finally going from the A14 up to the A16. While that's not quite as impressive as the M-series of chips, it is quick and powerful. On the Geekbench 6 test, it scored 6,233, falling quite a bit lower than the iPad Air and Pro. However, it still scored higher than most premium Chromebooks, so you can feel confident it can double as a workbook.

It has a solid screen size of 10.9 inches, providing plenty of room for those who want to use it as a drawing pad or journal, since it is compatible with the Apple Pencil USB-C and Apple Pencil 1st generation.

Overall, the iPad (11th generation) is a great entry-level tablet. It's best when working on a budget because if you're willing to spend a little more, the iPad Air is a better value.

Who is the iPad Mini for? That's a surprisingly hard question to answer. As our tech editor recently reviewed the newest Apple tablet and had this to say: "Let's cut to the chase: The iPad Mini is now my favorite tablet in the Apple lineup. It has an incredible display, excellent design, and strong performance. However, I'm not quite sure who should buy it."

That's because the iPad Mini is best for entertainment and casual users, but it carries a very non-casual price tag, making it harder to recommend, even though it's an undeniably great product.

Best for entertainment: If you're not interested in working on your iPad, then the iPad Mini is our top recommendation for you. Apple's mini tablet is now equipped with a gorgeous OLED display and a lighter, thinner design than the previous Mini. Even without an M-series chip, it still supports the new Siri AI and Apple Intelligence features. It's the perfect size for reading, and your Apple TV favorites will look fantastic on the OLED Super Retina XDR display, which boasts deep blacks and bright colors in all lighting conditions.

Upper-income shoppers: The new iPad Mini has an incredible display and fits just right in your hands, and there are so many things we love about it. We don't love the price tag, however. The $699 price a really big ask for most shoppers, especially if you're just using it to read, browse, and stream. But if cost isn't a concern, then we think you'll love this compact tablet, the lightest tablet Apple has ever made.

But let's put aside the price for a moment: The new iPad Mini is an undeniably good tablet.

Our tech editor said it has a "near-perfect design" that feels great in your hands. Apple also removed the speaker holes at the bottom of the design and introduced a new "Acoustic Display" system. Because of that, the newest iPad now has IP67 water and dust resistance. And the speakers sound great.

The OLED display does go a long way in justifying the premium price. While it lacks the ProMotion Display technology found in the iPad Pro or MacBook Pro OLED, its peak and standard brightness are impressive. Even the littlest details on the screen are razor-sharp, and the colors/blacks pop off the screen.

Professionals: Released in 2025, the current-gen iPad Pro is loaded with Apple's blazing-fast M5 chip, giving it way more power than the average user really needs. Graphic designers can store all of their creative work on the device, which has up to 2TB of storage. It's also a smart choice for video editors, though the premium nano-texture glass — which prevents glare while working in any environment, even full sun — will cost extra. Apple's legendary XDR Retina display, which combines super-bright tandem OLED with ProMotion technology, lets you play back HDR video with a high refresh rate.

Photo and video editors: For creative professionals, the Apple iPad Pro is a dream come true. It's also compatible with the Apple Pencil Pro, a tool that features a unique squeezable design and a barrel roll feature, enhancing precision and control in design work. The XDR Retina Display is so good that the iPad Pro is essentially a reference-quality monitor you can take anywhere.

While the iPad Air is going to be the best for most people, the iPad Pro levels things up for professionals.

The 2025 iPad Pro isn't just a great tablet, but an outstanding laptop. Loaded with Apple's latest M5 processing chip, it's one of the most advanced options from Apple. When running the Geekbench 6 tests, it scored between 16,200 and 16,400. That puts it in the top 8 percent of all laptops we've tested. That's miraculous levels of power for such a sleek, razor-thin package.

You can often find 1TB of storage in computers, but 2TB is rare in a laptop and nearly unheard of with a tablet, so it can store large files without a problem.

Apple has released a lot of new tablets in the past two years, but for college students, we have to recommend the most affordable option — the base-model iPad with the A16 chip. Why wait until Nov. 27 when T-Mobile is already offering one of the strongest mobile promos of the season?

We’ve rounded up all the best Black Friday deals from T-Mobile, but the clear standout is a limited-time offer that gets you the Apple iPhone 18 Pro for free (100% off its $1,199 list price). Apple rarely offers direct cash discounts on current-generation Pro models during Cyber Week, and certainly never takes $1,199 off an unlocked handset. That makes this early offer is best way to upgrade right now.

T-Mobile keeps things surprisingly flexible when it comes to qualifying for this deal:

Switch without a trade-in: Port your existing phone number from an eligible rival carrier to a qualifying T-Mix plan, and T-Mobile covers the full cost of the phone.

Trade in a phone in any condition: Already a T-Mobile customer or adding a new line without porting a number? You can still get the iPhone 18 Pro for free by trading in an eligible phone in any condition on a qualifying T-Mix plan.

T-Mobile distributes the full discount as 36 monthly bill credits. If you leave T-Mobile or cancel your line before that three-year payment plan wraps up, the credits stop immediately, and you will owe the remaining balance on the device.

You also won’t walk out of the checkout process without paying anything. You are still required to pay local sales tax on the phone's full $1,199.00 retail value upfront, plus T-Mobile's standard $35 device connection fee per line at checkout. You can't escape those upfront charges, even on "free" Black Friday phone promos.

Score the Apple iPhone 18 Pro for free this weekend with T-Mobile.

S&P 500 correction: Five forces behind the market sell-off

📅 2026-11-20  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Esteemed Wharton professor Jeremy Siegel was more animated than usual on Friday afternoon... with good reason.

The normally staid author of "Stocks for the Long Run" spoke to CNBC on Friday as the market careened toward some ugly milestones. The S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average closed in correction territory, off more than 10% from their records. The Nasdaq Composite lost its gain for the year and the more concentrated Nasdaq-100 entered bear market territory on an intraday basis.

"These things are like straws that break the camel's back. You know what I mean? Everyone [is] all, 'I don't care, I don't care cause everything else is good' and when those straws keep on going... Bang," said Siegel.

Just a month-and-a-half ago, the S&P 500 and the Nasdaq Composite were at record highs. But the straws were already adding up by then and a market in denial is just now recognizing them.

Here are the reasons behind the sell-off:

The breaking point came this week in the form of the initial public offering of artificial intelligence leader Anthropic and a pick-up in the selling intensity for one-time AI chip leader Nvidia.

Anthropic upped its offering amount a day before going public despite the already dimming climate around AI-related stocks. The move ensured it would be the biggest IPO ever, but played into the fears that this debut — following SpaceX's monster offering over the summer — was a collective cash-out moment for the AI complex that will leave the investing masses holding the bag.

In other words, it could be the ultimate "sell the news" moment for the AI trade.

On Thursday, Anthropic traded from its $83 IPO price to a high above $115 before instantly rolling over and taking the AI trade with it. Anthropic's sell-off intensified Friday, and when the dust settled Anthropic closed the week at $88.91.

The Nasdaq posted its worst week since the Covid plunge of March 2020, down 10%.

Nvidia fell 14% on Friday with the selling intensifying after a Reuters headline hit the tape that the chipmaker's accounting is the focus of a Securities and Exchange Commission probe.

Nvidia is now down nearly 40% in November alone, a month that saw the AI bull market's leading salesman, Jensen Huang, resign as its CEO because of an affair with an alleged Chinese spy that raised speculation about whether the company's chips were ending up in Beijing's hands despite government restrictions.

To start the week, the Commerce Department tightened restrictions on U.S.-made chips to stop China from accessing them remotely from other countries.

A solid earnings report on Tuesday fell flat as Nvidia's board definitively ruled out any chance Huang would return, disappointing some of the stock's fanatics still holding out hope for his far-fetched return. Nvidia ended the week with a 23% loss.

The S&P 500 traded below its 200-day moving average — what's considered a measure of a security's long-term trend — on Tuesday and made a convincing break of it on Thursday. This turned many on Wall Street— like commodity trading advisors — into automatic sellers on the market. So-called CTAs are individuals or organizations that advise others on trading futures and swaps across a range of assets.

"We know there's a lot of trend riders on these markets," said Siegel. "They have their trend lines. They've ridden it up and as soon as it goes down, they're selling and they're selling and they're selling and they just perpetuate" the decline, he said.

The Iran conflict has caused the price oil to surge and while it's off its highs it still remains above $83 a barrel, while the fees for transiting the Strait of Hormuz and the rest of the region keep persistent pressure on global prices.

A series of numbers earlier in the month showed inflation is still far from the Federal Reserve's 2% target, sparking a run of central bank officials to warn they may have to get more aggressive with interest rates.

After increasing rates in September by a quarter point, the Fed skipped a hike in late October and sparked concern among bond investors it may be falling behind the inflation fight. The 10-year Treasury yield subsequently jumped above 5% and stayed there until a flight of safe haven fixed income buying brought it back below that level on Friday.

Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller said this week that the Fed may need to do a half percentage point hike next month to catch up.

"I'm very, very concerned about inflation. It's kind of headed in the wrong direction, and it's spreading out," Waller told CNBC.

"Don't fight the Fed" has long been a maxim on Wall Street and right now expectations are that the central bank will need to raise rates in December and again in January.

And late-breaking on Friday was a report from the Financial Times that the Trump Administration is considering restarting strikes on Iran.

Before the grim market milestones were met on Friday, it had long lost its mojo on fears that the pace of growth driven by AI companies was slowing.

As Anthropic prepared to go public, its third-quarter figures released earlier this month showed revenue growth last quarter was 47%, down from 144% in the second quarter.

AI momentum trades, like the VanEck Semiconductor ETF and the iShares MSCI USA Momentum Factor ETF, all topped out during the summer.

The fears of slowing growth picked up this month after the Democrats won control of Congress and key governorships, fueling speculation that states — or even the whole country — could implement stricter rules on data center construction.

CoreWeave, which builds data centers using high amounts of debt, is down 71% from its high. Caterpillar, which has reinvented itself as an AI play because of data center construction and generators, is down more than 50% from its high.

The view of Siegel and many market observers is that the economy remains in good enough shape that this will just be a run-of-the-mill correction that resets a bull market that had raised people's expectations too high because of its early ferocity.

The S&P 500 returned more than 20% in 2023 and 2024 and then gained 16% last year.

"The average over long periods for the stock market is 8%, 9% a year up," said Siegel. "People began to think that 15 to 20 [percent] was going to be the norm with AI and we have to bring that down to reality."

The only problem with that theory is that the stock market and its wealth effect was one of the major factors underpinning the economy. Stocks are a greater source of household wealth — especially for baby boomers — than they ever have been before.

With the S&P 500 clinging to a 1% gain for the year, this stock market rout could end up tipping off a virtuous cycle that slows the economy, and in turn, leads to bigger equity losses.

Season Passes for a Venue With WooCommerce: Guest Passes and Cooldowns

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 20 • Originally published at pluginlab.dev

A season pass is not just a ticket with a longer expiry date. You need to decide who can use it, whether the holder can bring a guest, and what happens when someone presents the same code again at the entrance.

I built Tickets & Passes for WooCommerce with a separate Pass product type for admission across a period. It supports guest passes, cardholder photos and a cooldown between scans. Here is how I would plan a WooCommerce season pass for a venue without confusing admission with a broader membership system.

The plugin adds Ticket, Timeslot Ticket and Pass to WooCommerce's Product data dropdown. A Ticket is plain admission with a validity window and a set number of uses. A Timeslot Ticket is for a particular date and time, with capacity per slot. A Pass is valid across a period, which suits season passes and annual admission cards.

I would choose the product type from the admission rule, not the marketing name. If each visit must reserve a specific session, look at Timeslot Tickets instead. Do not assume that calling a product a membership makes it a reservation system.

The WordPress.org readme documents the setup. WooCommerce must already be active, and the phone scanner needs HTTPS for camera access.

Before selling publicly, I would make a test purchase and follow it through from checkout to check-in. Check the holder details, the validity you intended, and what staff actually see on their phone.

A pass can carry a cardholder photo collected at purchase. At check-in, staff see that photo beside the scan result and can compare it with the person at the door. The QR code identifies the pass; the photo gives staff another piece of information.

This is not a promise of automatic identity verification. I would write a simple entrance procedure explaining when staff should compare the photo and what to do if the person does not match. Test that procedure with the people who will use it, not just the administrator who configured the product.

A Pass can include guest passes that the holder gives away from their account page. That provides a guest route without treating the main holder's QR code as something everyone should share.

A cooldown does something different: it prevents the same pass code being scanned again immediately. The product also determines the maximum number of uses. I would decide those rules before advertising the pass, then test repeat entry against the intended policy.

Do not describe a cooldown as a complete sharing-prevention system. It is a rule about repeat scans. The cardholder photo, the guest allowance and the staff procedure address different parts of the admission process.

Customers find their passes in the Passes tab of WooCommerce My Account, with an on-screen QR code and a printable PDF. Live status shows whether a pass is active, used, expired, cancelled or not valid yet, along with uses remaining. WooCommerce order emails are extended with pass details.

At the entrance, staff log in to the scanner page, allow camera access and point it at the code. The scanner shows who it belongs to; staff tap Check In to confirm or Cancel to scan again. For an unreadable code, the scanner menu offers manual entry. The admin dashboards also provide manual check-in.

Every scan is checked against your server. The phone needs a connection, and the scanner does not work offline. I would test reception at the actual entrance rather than assume a successful office test covers the door.

Incorrect validity, use limits or cooldown rules can make the entrance behave differently from the promise on the product page. A missing connection prevents live validation, and camera access requires HTTPS. Test those conditions before a busy opening.

To stop a trial, deactivate the plugin. The readme says deactivation clears its scheduled tasks but leaves settings and records intact; reactivation picks up where you left off. Admission features stop while it is inactive, so this is not something to do while customers are relying on them.

Deletion is different: settings, scheduled tasks and the Scanner role are removed, while tickets, passes and check-in records are kept by default. The readme also documents an optional permanent-data deletion setting. Do not enable that to undo a trial; permanent removal cannot be undone. Keep a backup before testing removal.

If your membership requirement is recurring billing or access to restricted website content, this admission walkthrough is not evidence that those features are included. Choose tooling that explicitly documents the job you need. Likewise, a venue that requires offline scanning needs a different check-in approach.

For period-based admission sold through an existing WooCommerce shop, I would start with a Pass, test the holder and guest paths separately, and make sure the entrance rules match the offer before taking real orders.
